### Sexual orientation and gender identity

See also: [LGBTQ rights in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Illinois)

Chicago has the third-largest [lesbian, gay, bisexual, transgender, and queer](78174325.md) (LGBTQ) population in the United States.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] Since the 2013 legalization of [same-sex marriage in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Illinois), over 10,000 same-sex couples have wed in Illinois' [Cook County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County,_Illinois), a majority of them in Chicago.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213] Chicago is the first U.S. city to have a [pro-gay organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_for_Human_Rights) and consistently ranks as one of the most LGBTQ-friendly cities in the nation.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215] [Lake View](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_View,_Chicago) and [Edgewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewater,_Chicago) are historically the epicenters of [LGBTQ culture in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture_in_Chicago).[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217]&#x20;

### Religion

Religion in Chicago (2014)

[^cite_note-Religion1-218]

[^cite_note-Religion2-219]

1. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism) (35.0%)

2. [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism) (34.0%)

3. [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy) (1.00%)

4. [Jehovah's Witness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witness) (1.00%)

5. [No religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligious) (22.0%)

6. [Judaism](15624.md) (3.00%)

7. [Islam](6037917.md) (2.00%)

8. [Buddhism](3267529.md) (1.00%)

9. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) (1.00%)

According to a 2014 study by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), [Christianity](5211.md) is the most prevalently practiced religion in Chicago (71%),[^cite_note-Religion2-219] with the city being the fourth-most religious metropolis in the United States after [Dallas](53838.md), [Atlanta](3138.md) and [Houston](13774.md).[^cite_note-Religion2-219] [Roman Catholicism](410868.md) and [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_the_United_States) are the largest branches (34% and 35% respectively), followed by [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy) and [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses) with 1% each.[^cite_note-Religion1-218] Chicago also has a sizable non-Christian population. Non-Christian groups include [Irreligious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_the_United_States) (22%), [Judaism](1131183.md) (3%), [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_United_States) (2%), [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_the_United_States) (1%) and [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_United_States) (1%).[^cite_note-Religion1-218]&#x20;

Chicago is the headquarters of several religious denominations, including the [Evangelical Covenant Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Covenant_Church) and the [Evangelical Lutheran Church in America](248255.md). It is the seat of several [dioceses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocese_of_Chicago_\(disambiguation\)). The [Fourth Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Presbyterian_Church_\(Chicago\)) is one of the largest [Presbyterian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian) congregations in the United States based on memberships.[^cite_note-220] Since the 20th century Chicago has also been the headquarters of the [Assyrian Church of the East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_Church_of_the_East).[^cite_note-221] In 2014 the [Catholic Church](606848.md) was the largest individual Christian denomination (34%), with the [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Chicago) being the largest Catholic jurisdiction. [Evangelical Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Protestantism) form the largest theological Protestant branch (16%), followed by [Mainline Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) (11%), and historically [Black churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_church) (8%). Among denominational Protestant branches, [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists) formed the largest group in Chicago (10%); followed by Nondenominational (5%); [Lutherans](23371382.md) (4%); and [Pentecostals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostal) (3%).[^cite_note-Religion1-218]&#x20;

Non-Christian faiths accounted for 7% of the religious population in 2014. [Judaism](15624.md) has at least 261,000 adherents which is 3% of the population. A 2020 study estimated the total Jewish population of the Chicago metropolitan area, both religious and irreligious, at 319,500.[^cite_note-2020populationstudy.juf.org-189]&#x20;

The first two [Parliament of the World's Religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_World%27s_Religions) in 1893 and 1993 were held in Chicago.[^cite_note-222] Many international religious leaders have visited Chicago, including [Mother Teresa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Teresa), the [Dalai Lama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/14th_Dalai_Lama)[^cite_note-Watts2009-223] and [Pope John Paul II](23805.md) in 1979.[^cite_note-224] [Pope Leo XIV](54520372.md) was born in Chicago in 1955 and graduated from the [Catholic Theological Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Theological_Union) in [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park_\(Chicago\)).[^cite_note-225]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Chicago)

See also: [List of companies in the Chicago metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_in_the_Chicago_metropolitan_area)



[Federal Reserve Bank of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_Chicago)

Chicago has the third-largest [gross metropolitan product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product) in the United States—about \$670.5 billion according to September 2017 estimates.[^cite_note-226] The city has also been rated as having the most balanced economy in the United States, due to its high level of diversification.[^cite_note-227] The Chicago metropolitan area has the third-largest science and engineering work force of any metropolitan area in the nation.[^cite_note-washingtonpost.com-228] Chicago was the base of commercial operations for industrialists [John Crerar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Crerar_\(industrialist\)), [John Whitfield Bunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Whitfield_Bunn), [Richard Teller Crane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Teller_Crane), [Marshall Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Field), [John Farwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Farwell), [Julius Rosenwald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Rosenwald), and many other commercial visionaries who laid the foundation for Midwestern and global industry.&#x20;

Chicago is a major world financial center, with the second-largest central business district in the United States, following [Midtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Manhattan).[^cite_note-229] The city is the seat of the [Federal Reserve Bank of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_Chicago), the Bank's Seventh District. The city has major financial and [futures exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futures_exchange), including the [Chicago Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Stock_Exchange), the [Cboe Global Markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cboe_Global_Markets) Options Exchange, and the [Chicago Mercantile Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Mercantile_Exchange) (the "Merc"), which is owned, along with the [Chicago Board of Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Board_of_Trade) (CBOT), by Chicago's [CME Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CME_Group). In 2017, Chicago exchanges traded 4.7 billion in derivatives. [Chase Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chase_\(bank\)) has its commercial and retail banking headquarters in Chicago's [Chase Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chase_Tower_\(Chicago\)).[^cite_note-230] Academically, Chicago has been influential through the [Chicago school of economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_school_of_economics), which fielded 12 [Nobel Prize](23535306.md) winners.&#x20;



The [Chicago Board of Trade Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Board_of_Trade_Building)

The city and its surrounding metropolitan area contain the third-largest labor pool in the United States with about 4.63 million workers.[^cite_note-231] Illinois is home to 66 [*Fortune* 1000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_1000) companies, including those in Chicago.[^cite_note-232] The city of Chicago also hosts 12 *Fortune* Global 500 companies and 17 *Financial Times* 500 companies. The city claims three [Dow 30](47361.md) companies: [aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace) giant [Boeing](18933266.md), which moved its headquarters from [Seattle](11388236.md) to the Chicago Loop in 2001;[^cite_note-233] [McDonald's](2480627.md); and [Walgreens Boots Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walgreens_Boots_Alliance).[^cite_note-234] For six consecutive years from 2013 through 2018, Chicago was ranked the nation's top metropolitan area for corporate relocations.[^cite_note-235] However, three [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies left Chicago in 2022, leaving the city with 35, still second to New York City.[^cite_note-ChicagoBusinessExodus3-236]&#x20;

Manufacturing, printing, publishing, and food processing also play major roles in the city's economy. Several medical products and services companies are based in the Chicago area, including [Baxter International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baxter_International), [Boeing](18933266.md), [Abbott Laboratories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbott_Laboratories), and the Healthcare division of [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric). Prominent food companies based in Chicago include the world headquarters of [Conagra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conagra), [Ferrara Candy Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrara_Candy_Company), [Kraft Heinz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraft_Heinz), [McDonald's](2480627.md), [Mondelez International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mondelez_International), and [Quaker Oats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quaker_Oats).[^cite_note-237] Chicago has been a hub of the [retail](183515.md) sector since its early development, with [Montgomery Ward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_Ward), [Sears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sears), and [Marshall Field's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Field%27s). Today the Chicago metropolitan area is the headquarters of several retailers, including [Walgreens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walgreens), [Sears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sears_Holdings_Corporation), [Ace Hardware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ace_Hardware), [Claire's](596203.md), [ULTA Beauty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ULTA_Beauty), and [Crate & Barrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crate_%26_Barrel).[^cite_note-238] Tech companies based in the area include [CDW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CDW), Uber Frieght, [Zebra Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zebra_Technologies), [Motorola Solutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorola_Solutions), [Motorola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorola) along with regional offices for [Google](1092923.md), [Salesforce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesforce), [LinkedIn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LinkedIn), and more.&#x20;

Late in the 19th century, Chicago was part of the [bicycle craze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bike_boom#1890s), with the Western Wheel Company, which introduced [stamping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamping_\(metalworking\)) to the production process and significantly reduced costs,[^cite_note-239] while early in the 20th century, the city was part of the automobile revolution, hosting the [Brass Era car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brass_Era_car) builder Bugmobile, which was founded there in 1907.[^cite_note-240] Chicago was also the site of the [Schwinn Bicycle Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schwinn_Bicycle_Company).&#x20;

Chicago is a major world convention destination. The city's main convention center is McCormick Place. With its four interconnected buildings, it is the largest convention center in the nation and third-largest in the world.[^cite_note-241] Chicago also ranks third in the U.S. (behind [Las Vegas](47737.md) and [Orlando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando,_Florida)) in number of conventions hosted annually.[^cite_note-242]&#x20;

Chicago's minimum wage for non-tipped employees is one of the highest in the nation and reached \$15 in 2021.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244]&#x20;

## Culture and contemporary life

Main article: [Culture of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Chicago)

Further information: [List of people from Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_from_Chicago)



[Navy Pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy_Pier) in the [Streeterville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streeterville) neighborhood is one of the most visited attractions in the [Midwestern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States)

The city's waterfront location and nightlife attracts residents and tourists alike. Over a third of the city population is concentrated in the lakefront neighborhoods from [Rogers Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rogers_Park,_Chicago) in the north to [South Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Shore,_Chicago) in the south.[^cite_note-2000-demographics-map-245] The city has many upscale dining establishments as well as many ethnic restaurant districts. These districts include the [Mexican American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_American) neighborhoods, such as [Pilsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilsen,_Chicago) along 18th street, and *La Villita* along 26th Street; the [Puerto Rican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Ricans_in_Chicago) enclave of [Paseo Boricua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paseo_Boricua) in the [Humboldt Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humboldt_Park,_Chicago) neighborhood; [Greektown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greektown,_Chicago), along South [Halsted Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halsted_Street), immediately west of downtown;[^cite_note-246] [Little Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Italy,_Chicago), along Taylor Street; [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Chicago) in [Armour Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armour_Square,_Chicago); [Polish Patches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Patches) in [West Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Town,_Chicago); [Little Seoul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreatown#Chicago,_Illinois) in [Albany Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albany_Park,_Chicago) around Lawrence Avenue; [Little Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Vietnam,_Chicago) near [Broadway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadway_\(Chicago\)) in Uptown; and the [Desi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desi) area, along [Devon Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devon_Avenue_\(Chicago\)) in [West Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Ridge,_Chicago).[^cite_note-247]&#x20;

Downtown is the center of Chicago's financial, cultural, governmental, and commercial institutions and the site of Grant Park and many of the city's skyscrapers. Many of the city's financial institutions, such as the CBOT and the Federal Reserve Bank of Chicago, are located within a section of downtown called "The Loop", which is an eight-block by five-block area of city streets that is encircled by elevated rail tracks. The term "The Loop" is largely used by locals to refer to the entire downtown area as well. The central area includes the [Near North Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_North_Side,_Chicago), the [Near South Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_South_Side,_Chicago), and the [Near West Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Loop), as well as the Loop. These areas contribute famous [skyscrapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Chicago), abundant restaurants, [shopping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnificent_Mile), [museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Campus), [Soldier Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldier_Field), convention facilities, [parkland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chicago_parks), and [beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaches_in_Chicago).&#x20;



[Nature Boardwalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Boardwalk) at [Lincoln Park Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park_Zoo) on the North Side

Lincoln Park contains [Lincoln Park Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park_Zoo) and [Lincoln Park Conservatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park_Conservatory). The [River North Gallery District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_North_Gallery_District,_Near_North_Side,_Chicago) features the nation's largest concentration of contemporary art galleries outside of New York City.[^cite_note-248] [Lake View](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_View,_Chicago) is home to [Boystown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boystown,_Chicago), the city's large [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) nightlife and culture center. The [Chicago Pride Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Pride_Parade), held the last Sunday in June, is one of the world's largest with over a million people in attendance.[^cite_note-249] North [Halsted Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halsted_Street) is the main thoroughfare of Boystown.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;

The South Side neighborhood of [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_Chicago) is the home of former U.S. President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) and First Lady [Michelle Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Obama). The neighborhood is home to the [Barack Obama Presidential Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_Presidential_Center), a museum and library dedicated to his presidency.[^cite_note-251] It also contains the University of Chicago, ranked one of the world's top ten universities,[^cite_note-252] and the [Museum of Science and Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Science_and_Industry_\(Chicago\)). The 6-mile (9.7 km) long [Burnham Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burnham_Park_\(Chicago\)) stretches along the waterfront of the South Side. Two of the city's largest parks are also located on this side of the city: Jackson Park, bordering the waterfront, hosted the World's Columbian Exposition in 1893, and is the site of the aforementioned museum; and slightly west sits [Washington Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Park_\(Chicago_park\)). The two parks themselves are connected by a wide strip of parkland called the [Midway Plaisance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midway_Plaisance), running adjacent to the University of Chicago. The South Side hosts one of the city's largest parades, the annual African American [Bud Billiken Parade and Picnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bud_Billiken_Parade_and_Picnic), which travels through [Bronzeville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronzeville,_Chicago) to Washington Park. [Ford Motor Company](30433662.md) has an [automobile assembly plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Assembly) on the South Side in [Hegewisch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hegewisch,_Chicago), and most of the facilities of the Port of Chicago are also on the South Side.&#x20;

The West Side holds the [Garfield Park Conservatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garfield_Park_Conservatory), one of the largest collections of tropical plants in any U.S. city. Prominent Latino cultural attractions found here include [Humboldt Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humboldt_Park_\(Chicago_park\))'s [Institute of Puerto Rican Arts and Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Puerto_Rican_Arts_and_Culture) and the annual Puerto Rican People's Parade, as well as the [National Museum of Mexican Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Mexican_Art) and [St. Adalbert's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Adalbert%27s_in_Chicago) in [Pilsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilsen,_Chicago). The Near West Side holds the [University of Illinois at Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_at_Chicago) and was once home to [Oprah Winfrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oprah_Winfrey)'s [Harpo Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harpo_Studios), the site of which has been rebuilt as the global headquarters of McDonald's.[^cite_note-253]&#x20;

The city's distinctive accent, made famous by its use in classic films like *[The Blues Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Blues_Brothers_\(film\))* and television programs like the *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live)* skit "[Bill Swerski's Superfans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Swerski%27s_Superfans)", is an advanced form of [Inland Northern American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inland_Northern_American_English). This dialect can be found in other cities bordering the Great Lakes, such as [Detroit](8687.md), [Cleveland](5951.md), [Milwaukee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee), and [Buffalo, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo,_New_York), and most prominently features a rearrangement of certain vowel sounds, such as the [short "a"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phonological_history_of_English_short_A) sound as in "cat", which can sound more like "kyet" to outsiders. The accent remains well associated with the city.[^cite_note-254]&#x20;

### Entertainment and the arts

Further information: [Theater in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater_in_Chicago), [Visual arts of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_arts_of_Chicago), and [Music of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Chicago)

See also: [List of theaters in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_theaters_in_Chicago)



[Chicago Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Theatre)

Renowned Chicago theater companies include the [Goodman Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodman_Theatre) in the Loop; the [Steppenwolf Theatre Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppenwolf_Theatre_Company) and [Victory Gardens Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Gardens_Theater) in Lincoln Park; and the [Chicago Shakespeare Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Shakespeare_Theater) at Navy Pier. [Broadway In Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadway_In_Chicago) offers Broadway-style entertainment at five theaters: the [Nederlander Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nederlander_Theatre_\(Chicago\)), [CIBC Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIBC_Theatre), [Cadillac Palace Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadillac_Palace_Theatre), [Auditorium Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auditorium_Building) of [Roosevelt University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_University), and [Broadway Playhouse at Water Tower Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadway_Playhouse_at_Water_Tower_Place). [Polish language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language) productions for [Chicago's large Polish speaking population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poles_in_Chicago) can be seen at the historic [Gateway Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_Theatre_\(Chicago\)) in [Jefferson Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Park,_Chicago). Since 1968, the [Joseph Jefferson Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Jefferson_Award) are given annually to acknowledge excellence in theater in the Chicago area. Chicago's theater community spawned modern [improvisational theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improvisational_theater), and includes the prominent groups [The Second City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Second_City) and [I.O.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IO_Theater) (formerly ImprovOlympic).&#x20;

The [Chicago Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Symphony_Orchestra) (CSO) performs at [Symphony Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symphony_Center), and is recognized as one of the best orchestras in the world.[^cite_note-255] Also performing regularly at Symphony Center is the [Chicago Sinfonietta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Sinfonietta), a more diverse and multicultural counterpart to the CSO. In the summer, many outdoor concerts are given in Grant Park and [Millennium Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Park). [Ravinia Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ravinia_Festival), located 25 miles (40 km) north of Chicago, is the summer home of the CSO, and is a favorite destination for many Chicagoans. The [Civic Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_Opera_House_\(Chicago\)) is home to the [Lyric Opera of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyric_Opera_of_Chicago).[^cite_note-256] The [Lithuanian Opera Company of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Opera_Company_of_Chicago) was founded by [Lithuanian Chicagoans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanians_in_the_Chicago_area) in 1956,[^cite_note-lithopera-257] and presents operas in [Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_language).&#x20;

The [Joffrey Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joffrey_Ballet) and [Chicago Festival Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Festival_Ballet) perform in various venues, including the [Harris Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_Theater_\(Chicago,_Illinois\)) in [Millennium Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Park). Chicago has several other contemporary and jazz dance troupes, such as the [Hubbard Street Dance Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubbard_Street_Dance_Chicago) and [Chicago Dance Crash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Dance_Crash).&#x20;



[Jay Pritzker Pavilion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay_Pritzker_Pavilion)

Other live-music genre which are part of the city's cultural heritage include [Chicago blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_blues), [Chicago soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_soul), [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz), and [gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_music). The city is the birthplace of [house music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_music) (a popular form of electronic dance music) and [industrial music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_music), and is the site of an influential [hip hop scene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_hip_hop). In the 1980s and 90s, the city was the global center for house and industrial music, two forms of music created in Chicago, as well as being popular for [alternative rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_rock), [punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punk_rock), and [new wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_wave_music). The city has been a center for [rave](43488.md) culture, since the 1980s. A flourishing independent rock music culture brought forth Chicago [indie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_music). [Annual festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_festivals_in_Chicago) feature various acts, such as [Lollapalooza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lollapalooza) and the [Pitchfork Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitchfork_Music_Festival). Lollapalooza originated in 1991 as a touring festival, but as of 2005 its home has been Chicago.[^cite_note-258] A 2007 report on the Chicago music industry by the [University of Chicago Cultural Policy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_School_of_Public_Policy_Studies) ranked Chicago third among metropolitan U.S. areas in "size of music industry" and fourth among all U.S. cities in "number of concerts and performances".[^cite_note-259]&#x20;

Chicago has a distinctive [fine art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_art) tradition. For much of the twentieth century, it nurtured a strong style of figurative [surrealism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrealism), as in the works of [Ivan Albright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Albright) and [Ed Paschke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Paschke). In 1968 and 1969, members of the [Chicago Imagists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Imagists), such as [Roger Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Brown_\(artist\)), [Leon Golub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Golub), [Robert Lostutter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Lostutter), [Jim Nutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Nutt), and [Barbara Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Rossi_\(artist\)) produced bizarre representational paintings. [Henry Darger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Darger) is one of the most celebrated figures of [outsider art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outsider_art).[^cite_note-June_Skinner_Sawyers-260]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Chicago)

See also: [List of beaches in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_beaches_in_Chicago)



Ferries offer sightseeing tours and water-taxi transportation along the [Chicago River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_River) and [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan).

In 2014, Chicago attracted 50.17 million domestic leisure travelers, 11.09 million domestic business travelers and 1.308 million overseas visitors.[^cite_note-visitors-261] These visitors contributed more than US\$13.7 billion to Chicago's economy.[^cite_note-visitors-261] Upscale shopping along the Magnificent Mile and State Street, thousands of restaurants, as well as Chicago's architecture, continue to draw tourists. The city is the United States' third-largest [convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_\(meeting\)) destination. A 2017 study by [Walk Score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk_Score) ranked Chicago the sixth-most walkable of fifty largest cities in the United States.[^cite_note-262] Most conventions are held at McCormick Place, just south of [Soldier Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldier_Field). Navy Pier, located just east of [Streeterville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streeterville), is 3,000 ft (910 m) long and houses retail stores, restaurants, museums, exhibition halls and auditoriums. The Ferris wheel first appeared at the World's Columbian Exposition, held in Chicago in 1893.[^cite_note-263] The Willis Tower, formerly named Sears Tower, is a popular destination for tourists.[^cite_note-264]&#x20;

### Museums

Further information: [List of museums and cultural institutions in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_and_cultural_institutions_in_Chicago)



The [Field Museum of Natural History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_Museum_of_Natural_History)

Among the city's museums are the [Adler Planetarium & Astronomy Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adler_Planetarium_%26_Astronomy_Museum), the [Field Museum of Natural History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_Museum_of_Natural_History), and the [Shedd Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shedd_Aquarium). The [Museum Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Campus) joins the southern section of Grant Park, which includes the renowned [Art Institute of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Institute_of_Chicago). Buckingham Fountain anchors the downtown park along the lakefront. The University of Chicago's [Institute for the Study of Ancient Cultures, West Asia & North Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_Ancient_Cultures,_West_Asia_%26_North_Africa) has an extensive collection of [ancient Egyptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt) and [Near Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_East) archaeological artifacts.[^cite_note-auto1-265]&#x20;

Other museums and galleries in Chicago include the [Chicago History Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_History_Museum), the [Driehaus Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driehaus_Museum), the [DuSable Museum of African American History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuSable_Museum_of_African_American_History), the [Museum of Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Contemporary_Art,_Chicago), the [Peggy Notebaert Nature Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peggy_Notebaert_Nature_Museum), the [Polish Museum of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Museum_of_America), the [Museum of Broadcast Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Broadcast_Communications), the [Chicago Architecture Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Architecture_Foundation), and the [Museum of Science and Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Science_and_Industry_\(Chicago\)).[^cite_note-auto1-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267]&#x20;

### Cuisine

See also: [Culture of Chicago § Food and drink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Chicago#Food_and_drink), [Chicago farmers' markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_farmers%27_markets), and [List of Michelin-starred restaurants in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Michelin-starred_restaurants_in_Chicago)



A [Chicago-style deep-dish pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago-style_pizza)

Chicago lays claim to a large number of regional specialties that reflect the city's ethnic and working-class roots. Included among these are its nationally renowned [deep-dish pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago-style_pizza); this style is said to have originated at [Pizzeria Uno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uno_Chicago_Grill).[^cite_note-268] The Chicago-style thin crust is also popular in the city.[^cite_note-269] Certain Chicago pizza favorites include [Lou Malnati's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lou_Malnati%27s) and [Giordano's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giordano%27s).[^cite_note-270]&#x20;

The [Chicago-style hot dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago-style_hot_dog), typically an all-beef hot dog, is loaded with an array of toppings that often includes pickle relish, [yellow mustard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustard_\(condiment\)#Yellow_mustard), pickled [sport peppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_pepper), [tomato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomato) wedges, [dill pickle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dill_pickle) spear and topped off with [celery salt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celery_salt) on a [poppy seed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poppy_seed) [bun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_dog_bun).[^cite_note-271] Enthusiasts of the Chicago-style hot dog frown upon the use of [ketchup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ketchup) as a garnish, but may prefer to add [giardiniera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giardiniera).[^cite_note-Recipe_Detail:_Chicago_Style_Hot_Dog-272][^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274]&#x20;



A [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poles_in_Chicago) market in Chicago

A distinctly Chicago sandwich, the [Italian beef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_beef) sandwich is thinly sliced beef simmered in [au jus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Au_jus) and served on an Italian roll with sweet peppers or spicy giardiniera. A popular modification is the Combo—an Italian beef sandwich with the addition of an Italian sausage. The [Maxwell Street Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxwell_Street_Polish) is a grilled or deep-fried [kielbasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kielbasa)—on a hot dog roll, topped with grilled onions, yellow mustard, and hot sport peppers.[^cite_note-sandwich-275]&#x20;

[Chicken Vesuvio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicken_Vesuvio) is roasted bone-in chicken cooked in oil and garlic next to garlicky oven-roasted potato wedges and a sprinkling of green peas. The [Puerto Rican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Puerto_Rico)-influenced [jibarito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jibarito) is a sandwich made with flattened, fried green plantains instead of bread. The [mother-in-law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother-in-law_\(sandwich\)) is a [tamale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamale) topped with chili and served on a hot dog bun.[^cite_note-276] The tradition of serving the [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_cuisine) dish [saganaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saganaki) while aflame has its origins in Chicago's Greek community.[^cite_note-277] The appetizer, which consists of a square of fried cheese, is doused with [Metaxa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metaxa) and [flambéed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flamb%C3%A9ed) table-side.[^cite_note-278] [Chicago-style barbecue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago-style_barbecue) features hardwood smoked [rib tips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rib_tips) and [hot links](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_links) which were traditionally cooked in an aquarium smoker, a Chicago invention.[^cite_note-279] [Annual festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_festivals_in_Chicago) feature various Chicago signature dishes, such as [Taste of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taste_of_Chicago) and the Chicago Food Truck Festival.[^cite_note-280]&#x20;

One of the world's most decorated restaurants and a recipient of three [Michelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelin_Guide) stars, [Alinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alinea_\(restaurant\)) is located in Chicago. Well-known chefs who have had restaurants in Chicago include: [Charlie Trotter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Trotter), [Rick Tramonto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Tramonto), [Grant Achatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grant_Achatz), and [Rick Bayless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Bayless). In 2003, *[Robb Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robb_Report)* named Chicago the country's "most exceptional dining destination".[^cite_note-281]&#x20;

### Literature

Main article: [Chicago literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_literature)

Chicago literature finds its roots in the city's tradition of lucid, direct journalism, lending to a strong tradition of [social realism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_realism). In the *[Encyclopedia of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclopedia_of_Chicago)*, [Northwestern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_University) Professor Bill Savage describes Chicago fiction as prose which tries to "capture the essence of the city, its spaces and its people." The challenge for early writers was that Chicago was a frontier outpost that transformed into a global metropolis in the span of two generations.[^cite_note-auto2-282]&#x20;

Narrative fiction of that time, much of it in the style of "high-flown romance" and "genteel realism", needed a new approach to describe the urban social, political, and economic conditions of Chicago.[^cite_note-auto2-282] Nonetheless, Chicagoans worked hard to create a literary tradition that would stand the test of time,[^cite_note-283] and create a "city of feeling" out of concrete, steel, vast lake, and open prairie.[^cite_note-284] Much notable Chicago fiction focuses on the city itself, with social criticism keeping exultation in check.&#x20;

At least three short periods in the [history of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Chicago) have had a lasting influence on [American literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_literature).[^cite_note-285] These include from the time of the Great Chicago Fire to about 1900, what became known as the Chicago Literary Renaissance in the 1910s and early 1920s, and the period of the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression) to the 1940s.&#x20;

What would become the influential *[Poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poetry_\(magazine\))* magazine was founded in 1912 by [Harriet Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harriet_Monroe), who was working as an [art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art) [critic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critic) for the *Chicago Tribune*. The magazine discovered such poets as [Gwendolyn Brooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwendolyn_Brooks), [James Merrill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Merrill), and [John Ashbery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Ashbery).[^cite_note-ny-286] [T. S. Eliot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T._S._Eliot)'s first professionally published poem, "[The Love Song of J. Alfred Prufrock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Love_Song_of_J._Alfred_Prufrock)", was first published by *Poetry*. Contributors have included [Ezra Pound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ezra_Pound), [William Butler Yeats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Butler_Yeats), [William Carlos Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Carlos_Williams), [Langston Hughes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langston_Hughes), and [Carl Sandburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Sandburg), among others. The magazine was instrumental in launching the [Imagist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imagist) and [Objectivist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Objectivist_poets) poetic movements. From the 1950s to the 1970s, American poetry continued to evolve in Chicago.[^cite_note-287] In the 1980s, a modern form of poetry performance began in Chicago, the [poetry slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poetry_slam).[^cite_note-288]&#x20;

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Chicago)



[Soldier Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldier_Field)



[Wrigley Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrigley_Field)



[United Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Center)



[Rate Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_Field)

The city has two [Major League Baseball](38776.md) (MLB) teams: the [Chicago Cubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Cubs) of the National League play in Wrigley Field on the North Side; and the [Chicago White Sox](5945.md) of the American League play in Rate Field on the South Side. The two teams have faced each other in a World Series only once, in 1906.[^cite_note-289]&#x20;

The Cubs are the oldest Major League Baseball team to have never changed their city;[^cite_note-290] they have played in Chicago since 1871.[^cite_note-291] They had the dubious honor of having the longest championship drought in American professional sports, failing to win a World Series between 1908 and 2016. The White Sox have played on the South Side continuously since 1901. They have won three World Series titles (1906, 1917, 2005) and six American League pennants, including the first in 1901.&#x20;

The [Chicago Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bears), one of the last two remaining charter members of the [National Football League](21211.md) (NFL), have won nine [NFL Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NFL_champions), including the 1985 [Super Bowl XX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XX). The Bears play their home games at Soldier Field.&#x20;

The [Chicago Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bulls) of the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) (NBA) is one of the most recognized basketball teams in the world.[^cite_note-292] During the 1990s, with [Michael Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jordan) leading them, the Bulls won six NBA championships in eight seasons.[^cite_note-bulls_dynasty-293][^cite_note-294]&#x20;

The [Chicago Blackhawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Blackhawks) of the [National Hockey League](21809.md) (NHL) began play in 1926, and are one of the "[Original Six](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_Six)" teams of the NHL. The Blackhawks have won six [Stanley Cups](66968.md), including in 2010, 2013, and [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Stanley_Cup_Finals). Both the Bulls and the Blackhawks play at the [United Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Center).[^cite_note-295]&#x20;
