The [Chicago Half Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Half_Marathon) on [Lake Shore Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Shore_Drive) on the South Side

[Chicago Fire FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Fire_FC) is a member of [Major League Soccer](71802.md) (MLS) and plays at Soldier Field. The Fire have won one league title and four [U.S. Open Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamar_Hunt_U.S._Open_Cup), since their founding in 1997. In 1994, the United States hosted a successful [FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup) with games played at Soldier Field.[^cite_note-296]&#x20;

The [Chicago Stars FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Stars_FC) are a team in the [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women%27s_Soccer_League) (NWSL). They previously played in [Women's Professional Soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_Professional_Soccer) (WPS), of which they were a founding member, before joining the NWSL in 2013. They play at [SeatGeek Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SeatGeek_Stadium) in [Bridgeview, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridgeview,_Illinois).&#x20;

The [Chicago Sky](2717051.md) is a professional basketball team playing in the [Women's National Basketball Association](57721.md) (WNBA). They play home games at the [Wintrust Arena](40651728.md). The team was founded before the 2006 WNBA season began.[^cite_note-297]&#x20;

The [Chicago Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Marathon) has been held each year since 1977 except for 1987, when a half marathon was run in its place. The Chicago Marathon is one of six [World Marathon Majors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Marathon_Majors).[^cite_note-298]&#x20;

[Chicagoland Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicagoland_Speedway) in nearby [Joliet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joliet,_Illinois) has hosted [NASCAR Cup Series](56571945.md) races since [its opening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Tropicana_400) in [2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_NASCAR_Winston_Cup_Series), the track also hosts the [O'Reilly Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR_O%27Reilly_Auto_Parts_Series), formerly the [Truck Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR_Craftsman_Truck_Series), and the [ARCA Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ARCA_Menards_Series), [IndyCar](30446232.md) also ran at the track until [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_IndyCar_Series), the [2002 Delphi Indy 300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Delphi_Indy_300) was the closest finish in [IndyCar Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyCar_Series) history. The [Chicago Street Course](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Street_Course) in [downtown Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Loop) had races in the Cup Series and [Xfinity Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR_O%27Reilly_Auto_Parts_Series) from 2023 to 2025, while the often forgotten [Chicago Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Motor_Speedway) held [CART](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Championship_Auto_Racing_Teams) and NASCAR Truck Series races.&#x20;

Five area colleges play in [Division I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_I_\(NCAA\)) conferences: two from major conferences—the [DePaul Blue Demons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DePaul_Blue_Demons) ([Big East Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference)) and the [Northwestern Wildcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Wildcats) ([Big Ten Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Ten_Conference))—and three from other D1 conferences—the [Chicago State Cougars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_State_Cougars) ([Northeast Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Conference)); the [Loyola Ramblers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyola_Ramblers) ([Atlantic 10 Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_10_Conference)); and the [UIC Flames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UIC_Flames) ([Missouri Valley Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Valley_Conference)).[^cite_note-299]&#x20;

Chicago has also entered into [esports](564204.md) with the creation of the [OpTic Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpTic_Chicago), a professional [Call of Duty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Call_of_Duty) team that participates within the [CDL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Call_of_Duty_League).[^cite_note-300]&#x20;

## Parks and greenspace

Main articles: [Parks in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parks_in_Chicago), [Chicago Boulevard System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Boulevard_System), and [Cook County Forest Preserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County_Forest_Preserves)



[Buckingham Fountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckingham_Fountain) is in [Grant Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grant_Park_\(Chicago\)) in the [Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Loop).

When Chicago was incorporated in 1837, it chose the motto *Urbs in Horto*, a [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin) phrase which means "City in a Garden". Today, the [Chicago Park District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Park_District) consists of more than 570 parks with over 8,000 acres (3,200 ha) of [municipal parkland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_park). There are 31 sand [beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_beaches_in_Chicago,_Illinois), a plethora of museums, two world-class conservatories, and 50 nature areas.[^cite_note-301] Lincoln Park, the largest of the city's parks, covers 1,200 acres (490 ha) and has over 20 million visitors each year, making it third in the number of visitors after [Central Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park) in [New York City](645042.md), and the [National Mall and Memorial Parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Mall_and_Memorial_Parks) in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)[^cite_note-302]&#x20;

There is a historic [boulevard system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_boulevard_system),[^cite_note-303] a network of wide, tree-lined [boulevards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulevard) which connect a number of Chicago [parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parks_in_Chicago).[^cite_note-WTTW-304] The boulevards and the parks were authorized by the Illinois legislature in 1869.[^cite_note-305] A number of [Chicago neighborhoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighborhoods_of_Chicago) emerged along these roadways in the 19th century.[^cite_note-WTTW-304] The building of the boulevard system continued intermittently until 1942. It includes nineteen boulevards, eight parks, and six [squares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_square), along twenty-six miles of interconnected streets.[^cite_note-306] The *Chicago Park Boulevard System Historic District* was listed on the [National Register of Historic Places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places) in 2018.[^cite_note-weekly-307][^cite_note-NR2018-308]&#x20;

With berths for more than 6,000 boats, the Chicago Park District operates the nation's largest municipal harbor system.[^cite_note-309] In addition to ongoing beautification and renewal projects for the existing parks, a number of new parks have been added in recent years, such as the [Ping Tom Memorial Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ping_Tom_Memorial_Park) in Chinatown, [DuSable Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuSable_Park_\(Chicago\)) on the Near North Side, and most notably, Millennium Park, which is in the northwestern corner of one of Chicago's oldest parks, Grant Park in the Chicago Loop.&#x20;

The wealth of greenspace afforded by Chicago's parks is further augmented by the [Cook County Forest Preserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County_Forest_Preserves), a network of open spaces containing forest, [prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie), [wetland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wetland), streams, and lakes that are set aside as natural areas which lie along the city's outskirts,[^cite_note-310] including both the [Chicago Botanic Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Botanic_Garden) in [Glencoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glencoe,_Illinois) and the [Brookfield Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookfield_Zoo) in [Brookfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookfield,_Illinois).[^cite_note-311] Washington Park is also one of the city's biggest parks; covering nearly 400 acres (160 ha). The park is listed on the [National Register of Historic Places listings in South Side Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places_listings_in_South_Side_Chicago).[^cite_note-312]&#x20;

## Law and government

### Government

Main article: [Government of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Chicago)



[Daley Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daley_Plaza) and the [Chicago Picasso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Picasso), with [City Hall-County Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Hall-County_Building_\(Chicago\)) visible in background. At right, the [Daley Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_J._Daley_Center) contains the state law courts.

The government of the City of Chicago is divided into executive and [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislature) branches. The [mayor of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Chicago) is the chief executive, elected by general election for a term of four years, with no term limits. The incumbent mayor is [Brandon Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandon_Johnson). The mayor appoints commissioners and other officials who oversee the various departments. As well as the mayor, Chicago's clerk and treasurer are also elected citywide. The [City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_City_Council) is the legislative branch and is made up of 50 [alderpersons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alderman#United_States), one elected from each [ward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wards_of_the_United_States) in the city.[^cite_note-313] The council takes official action through the passage of [ordinances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_ordinance) and resolutions and approves the city budget.[^cite_note-314]&#x20;

The [Chicago Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Police_Department) provides law enforcement and the [Chicago Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Fire_Department) provides fire suppression and emergency medical services for the city and its residents. Civil and criminal law cases are heard in the [Cook County Circuit Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County_Circuit_Court) of the State of Illinois court system, or in the [Northern District of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_District_of_Illinois), in the federal system. In the state court, the public prosecutor is the Illinois [state's attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State%27s_attorney); in the Federal court it is the United States [attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Attorney).&#x20;

### Politics

Main article: [Political history of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_history_of_Chicago)

| Year | [Democratic](5043544.md) | [Republican](32070.md) | Others |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_Illinois)** | **78.3%** *775,699* | 20.6% *203,817* | 1.2% *11,776* |
| **[2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election_in_Illinois)** | **82.5%** *944,735* | 15.8% *181,234* | 1.6% *18,772* |
| **[2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election_in_Illinois)** | **82.9%** *912,945* | 12.3% *135,320* | 4.8% *53,262* |

During much of the last half of the 19th century, Chicago's politics were dominated by a growing [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County_Democratic_Party) organization. During the 1880s and 1890s, Chicago had a powerful radical tradition with large and highly organized [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist), [anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchist) and [labor organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_organization).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchneirov1998173–174-316] For much of the 20th century, Chicago has been among the largest and most reliable Democratic strongholds in the United States; with Chicago's Democratic vote the state of Illinois has been "[solid blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_states_and_blue_states)" in [presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election) since 1992. Even before then, it was not unheard of for Republican presidential candidates to win handily in downstate Illinois, only to lose statewide due to large Democratic margins in Chicago.&#x20;

The citizens of Chicago have not elected a [Republican](32070.md) mayor since 1927, when [William Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Hale_Thompson) was voted into office. The strength of the party in Chicago is partly a consequence of Illinois state politics, where the Republicans have come to represent rural and farm concerns while the Democrats support urban issues such as Chicago's public school funding.&#x20;

Chicago contains less than 25% of the state's population, but it is split between eight of Illinois' 17 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois%27s_congressional_districts) in the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md). All eight of the city's representatives are Democrats; only two Republicans have represented a significant portion of the city since 1973, for one term each: [Robert P. Hanrahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_P._Hanrahan) from 1973 to 1975, and [Michael Patrick Flanagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Patrick_Flanagan) from 1995 to 1997.&#x20;

[Machine politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_politics) persisted in Chicago after the decline of similar machines in other large U.S. cities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontejano199933–34-317] During much of that time, the city administration found opposition mainly from a liberal "independent" faction of the Democratic Party. The independents finally gained control of city government in 1983 with the election of [Harold Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harold_Washington) (in office 1983–1987). From 1989 until May 16, 2011, Chicago was under the leadership of its longest-serving mayor, [Richard M. Daley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_M._Daley), the son of Richard J. Daley. Because of the dominance of the Democratic Party in Chicago, the Democratic [primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partisan_primary) vote held in the spring is generally more significant than the general elections in November for U.S. House and Illinois State seats. The aldermanic, mayoral, and other city offices are filled through nonpartisan elections with runoffs as needed.[^cite_note-318]&#x20;

The city is home of former United States President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) and First Lady [Michelle Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Obama); Barack Obama was formerly a state legislator representing Chicago and later a U.S. senator. The Obamas' residence is located near the University of Chicago in [Kenwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenwood,_Chicago) on the city's south side.[^cite_note-319]&#x20;

### Crime

Main article: [Crime in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_Chicago)

See also: [Timeline of organized crime in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_organized_crime_in_Chicago)



A [Ford Explorer](540317.md) [SUV](47696.md) as a [Chicago Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Police_Department) vehicle, 2021

Chicago's crime rate in 2020 was 3,926 per 100,000 people.[^cite_note-320] Chicago experienced major rises in [violent crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violent_crime) in the 1920s, in the late 1960s, and in the 2020s.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-Chicago_Police_Annual_Report_2017-322] Chicago's biggest [criminal justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_justice) challenges have changed little over the last 50 years, and statistically reside with homicide, [armed robbery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_robbery), gang violence, and [aggravated battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aggravated_battery). Chicago has a higher murder rate than the larger cities of New York and Los Angeles. However, while it has a large absolute number of crimes due to its size, Chicago is not among the top-25 most violent cities in the United States.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]&#x20;

Murder rates in Chicago vary greatly depending on the neighborhood in question.[^cite_note-325] The neighborhoods of [Englewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Englewood,_Chicago) on the South Side, and [Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin,_Chicago) on the West side, for example, have homicide rates that are ten times higher than other parts of the city.[^cite_note-326] Chicago has an estimated population of over 100,000 active gang members from nearly 60 factions.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328] According to reports in 2013, "most of Chicago's violent crime comes from gangs trying to maintain control of drug-selling territories,"[^cite_note-bloomberg1-329] and is specifically related to the activities of the [Sinaloa Cartel](16881931.md), which is active in several American cities.[^cite_note-330]&#x20;

Violent crime rates vary significantly by area of the city, with more economically developed areas having low rates, but other sections have much higher rates of crime.[^cite_note-bloomberg1-329] In 2013, the violent crime rate was 910 per 100,000 people;[^cite_note-331] the murder rate was 10.4 per 100,000 – while high crime districts saw 38.9 murders, low crime districts saw 2.5 murders per 100,000.[^cite_note-332]&#x20;

Chicago's long history of public [corruption](3608404.md) regularly draws the attention of federal law enforcement and federal prosecutors.[^cite_note-333] From 2012 to 2019, 33 Chicago alderpersons were convicted on corruption charges, roughly one third of those elected in the time period. A report from the Office of the Legislative Inspector General noted that over half of Chicago's elected alderpersons took illegal campaign contributions in 2013.[^cite_note-334] Most corruption cases in Chicago are prosecuted by the [U.S. Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Attorney)'s office, as legal [jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurisdiction) makes most offenses punishable as a federal crime.[^cite_note-335]&#x20;

## Education

### Schools and libraries



When it was opened in 1991, the central [Harold Washington Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harold_Washington_Library) appeared in *[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records)* as the largest municipal public library building in the world.

[Chicago Public Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Public_Schools) (CPS) is the governing body of the [school district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_district) that contains over 600 public elementary and high schools citywide, including several selective-admission magnet schools. There are eleven selective enrollment high schools in the Chicago Public Schools, designed to meet the needs of Chicago's most academically advanced students. These schools offer a rigorous curriculum with mainly honors and [Advanced Placement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_Placement) (AP) courses.[^cite_note-336] [Walter Payton College Prep High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Payton_College_Prep_High_School) is ranked number one in the city of Chicago and the state of Illinois.[^cite_note-337]&#x20;

Chicago high school rankings are determined by the average test scores on state achievement tests.[^cite_note-338] The district, with an enrollment exceeding 400,545 students (2013–2014 20th Day Enrollment), is the third-largest in the U.S.[^cite_note-339] In September 2012, teachers for the Chicago Teachers Union went on strike for the first time since 1987 over pay, resources, and other issues.[^cite_note-340] According to 2014 data, Chicago's "choice system", where students who test or apply and may attend one of approximately 130 public high schools, sorts students of different achievement levels into different schools (high performing, middle performing, and low performing schools).[^cite_note-341]&#x20;

Chicago has a network of [Lutheran schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_school),[^cite_note-342] and several private schools are run by other denominations and faiths, such as the [Ida Crown Jewish Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ida_Crown_Jewish_Academy) in [West Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Ridge,_Chicago). The [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Chicago) operates [Catholic schools](1452366.md), including [Jesuit preparatory schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Jesuit_secondary_schools_in_the_United_States#Illinois) and others. A number of private schools are completely secular. There is also the private [Chicago Academy for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Academy_for_the_Arts), a high school focused on six different artistic disciplines, and the public [Chicago High School for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_High_School_for_the_Arts), a high school focused on five disciplines (visual arts, theatre, musical theatre, dance, and music).[^cite_note-343]&#x20;

The [Chicago Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Public_Library) system operates three regional libraries and 77 neighborhood branches, including the central library.[^cite_note-344]&#x20;

### Colleges and universities

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of colleges and universities in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_Chicago).



The [University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago) campus as seen from the [Midway Plaisance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midway_Plaisance)

Since the 1850s, Chicago has been a world center of higher education and research with several universities. These institutions consistently rank among the top "National Universities" in the United States, as determined by *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_%26_World_Report)*.[^cite_note-345] Highly regarded universities in Chicago and the surrounding area are the University of Chicago; Northwestern University; [Illinois Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Institute_of_Technology); [Loyola University Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyola_University_Chicago); [DePaul University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DePaul_University); [Columbia College Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_College_Chicago) and the [University of Illinois Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Chicago). Other notable schools include: [Chicago State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_State_University); the [School of the Art Institute of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_of_the_Art_Institute_of_Chicago); [East–West University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East%E2%80%93West_University); [National Louis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Louis_University); [North Park University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Park_University); [Northeastern Illinois University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_Illinois_University); [Robert Morris University Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Morris_University_Illinois); [Roosevelt University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_University); [Saint Xavier University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Xavier_University); [Rush University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rush_University); and [Shimer College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimer_College).[^cite_note-346]&#x20;

[William Rainey Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Rainey_Harper), the first president of the University of Chicago, was instrumental in the creation of the [junior college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_college) concept, establishing nearby [Joliet Junior College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joliet_Junior_College) as the first in the nation in 1901.[^cite_note-347] His legacy continues with the multiple [community colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_college) in the Chicago proper, including the seven [City Colleges of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Colleges_of_Chicago): [Richard J. Daley College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_J._Daley_College), [Kennedy–King College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy%E2%80%93King_College), [Malcolm X College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malcolm_X_College), [Olive–Harvey College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive%E2%80%93Harvey_College), [Truman College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truman_College), [Harold Washington College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harold_Washington_College), and [Wilbur Wright College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilbur_Wright_College), in addition to the privately held [MacCormac College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MacCormac_College).&#x20;

Chicago also has a high concentration of post-baccalaureate institutions, graduate schools, seminaries, and theological schools, such as the [Adler School of Professional Psychology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adler_School_of_Professional_Psychology), [The Chicago School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Chicago_School) the [Erikson Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erikson_Institute), [Institute for Clinical Social Work](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_Clinical_Social_Work), [Lutheran School of Theology at Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_School_of_Theology_at_Chicago), [Catholic Theological Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Theological_Union), [Moody Bible Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moody_Bible_Institute), and [University of Chicago Divinity School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago_Divinity_School).&#x20;

## Media

Main article: [Media in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Chicago)

See also: [Chicago International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_International_Film_Festival)



[WGN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGN_\(AM\)) began in the early days of radio and developed into a multi-platform broadcaster, including a cable television super-station.



Chicago was home of *[The Oprah Winfrey Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Oprah_Winfrey_Show)* from 1986 until 2011, and of other Harpo Production operations until 2015.

### Television

The Chicago metropolitan area is a major media hub and the third-largest media market in the United States, after New York City and Los Angeles.[^cite_note-348] Each of the big five [U.S. television networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_over-the-air_television_networks), [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC), [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company), [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS), [Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company) and [The CW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_CW), directly owns and operates a [high-definition television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-definition_television) station in Chicago ([WMAQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WMAQ-TV) 5, [WLS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WLS-TV) 7, [WBBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBBM-TV) 2, [WFLD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFLD) 32 and [WGN-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGN-TV) 9, respectively). WGN is owned by the CW through a majority stake held in the network by the [Nexstar Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nexstar_Media_Group), which acquired it from its founding owner [Tribune Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribune_Broadcasting) in 2019. WGN was once carried, with some programming differences, as "[WGN America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGN_America)" on [cable and satellite TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cable_television) nationwide and in parts of the [Caribbean](18956035.md). WGN America eventually became [NewsNation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NewsNation) in 2021.&#x20;

Chicago has been the home of several prominent talk shows, including *[The Oprah Winfrey Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Oprah_Winfrey_Show)*, *[Steve Harvey Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Harvey_\(talk_show\))*, *[The Rosie Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rosie_Show)*, *[The Jerry Springer Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerry_Springer_Show)*, *[The Phil Donahue Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Phil_Donahue_Show)*, *[The Jenny Jones Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jenny_Jones_Show)*, and more. The city has one [PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS) member station (its second: [WYCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WYCC) 20, removed its affiliation with PBS in 2017[^cite_note-349]): [WTTW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTTW) 11, producer of shows such as *[Sneak Previews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sneak_Previews)*, *[The Frugal Gourmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Frugal_Gourmet)*, *[Lamb Chop's Play-Along](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_Chop%27s_Play-Along)* and *[The McLaughlin Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_McLaughlin_Group)*.&#x20;

As of 2018, *[Windy City Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windy_City_Live)* is Chicago's only daytime talk show, which is hosted by Val Warner and [Ryan Chiaverini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Chiaverini) at ABC7 Studios with a live weekday audience. Since 1999, *[Judge Mathis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judge_Mathis)* also films his syndicated arbitration-based reality [court show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_show) at the [NBC Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_Tower). Beginning in January 2019, *[Newsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsy)* began producing 12 of its 14 hours of live news programming per day from its new facility in Chicago.&#x20;

#### Television stations

Most of Chicago's television stations are owned and operated by the big television network companies. They are:&#x20;

- [WBBM-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBBM-TV) (2), owned and operated by [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS).

- [WMAQ-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WMAQ-TV) (5), owned and operated by [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC).

- [WLS-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WLS-TV) (7), owned and operated by [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company).

- [WGN-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGN-TV) (9), a [CW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_CW) station owned and operated by network majority owner [Nexstar Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nexstar_Media_Group).

- [WTTW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTTW) (11), a [PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS) member station owned by Window to the World Communications, Inc.

- [WCIU-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCIU-TV) (26), an independent station (with [MeTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MeTV) via low-powered [WWME-CD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WWME-CD) on DT3) owned and operated by [Weigel Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weigel_Broadcasting).

- [WFLD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFLD) (32), owned and operated by [Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company).

- [WWTO-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WWTO-TV) (35), owned and operated by TBN, licensed in [Naperville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naperville).

- [WCPX-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCPX-TV) (38), owned and operated by [Ion Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Television).

- [WSNS-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSNS-TV) (44), owned and operated by [Telemundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telemundo).

- [WPWR-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WPWR-TV) (50), owned and operated by [MyNetworkTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyNetworkTV) ([Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company)), licensed to [Gary, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary,_Indiana).

- [WYIN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WYIN) (56), a PBS member station owned by Northwest Indiana Public Broadcasting, Inc., licensed in Gary, Indiana.

- [WTVK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTVK_\(TV\)) (59), an independent station owned by Venture Technologies Group, licensed in [Oswego, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oswego,_Illinois).

- [WXFT-DT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WXFT-DT) (60), owned and operated by [Unimas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unimas).

- [WJYS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WJYS) (62), an independent station owned by Millennial Telecommunications, Inc., licensed to [Hammond, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hammond,_Indiana).

- [WGBO-DT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGBO-DT) (66), owned and operated by [Univision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision).

### Newspapers

Two major daily newspapers are published in Chicago: the *[Chicago Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Tribune)* and the *[Chicago Sun-Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Sun-Times)*, with the Tribune having the larger circulation. There are also several regional and special-interest newspapers and magazines, such as *[Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_\(magazine\))*, the [*Dziennik Związkowy* (*Polish Daily News*)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dziennik_Zwi%C4%85zkowy_\(Polish_Daily_News\)), *[Draugas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Draugas)* (the Lithuanian daily newspaper), the *[Chicago Reader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Reader)*, the *[SouthtownStar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SouthtownStar)*, the *[Chicago Defender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Defender)*, the *[Daily Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Herald_\(Arlington_Heights\))*, *[Newcity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newcity)*,[^cite_note-twsOctW11-350][^cite_note-twsOctW14-351] *[StreetWise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/StreetWise)* and the *[Windy City Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windy_City_Times)*.&#x20;

The entertainment and cultural magazine *[Time Out Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Out_Chicago)* and [*GRAB*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grab_\(magazine\)) magazine are published in the city, as well as local music magazine *[Chicago Innerview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Innerview)*. Chicago is the home of satirical national news outlet, *[The Onion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Onion)*, and its sister pop-culture publication, *[The A.V. Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_A.V._Club)*.[^cite_note-352]&#x20;

### Movies and filming

See also: [List of movies set in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_fiction_set_in_Chicago#Films) and [List of television shows set in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_fiction_set_in_Chicago#Television_shows)

### Radio

Chicago has five [50,000 watt AM radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_50_kW_AM_radio_stations_in_the_United_States): the [Audacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audacy,_Inc.)-owned [WBBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBBM_\(AM\)) and [WSCR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSCR); the [Tribune Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribune_Broadcasting)-owned [WGN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGN_\(AM\)); the [Cumulus Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumulus_Media)-owned [WLS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WLS_\(AM\)); and the [ESPN Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_Radio)-owned [WMVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WMVP). Chicago is also home to a number of national radio shows, including *[Beyond the Beltway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyond_the_Beltway)* with [Bruce DuMont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_DuMont) on Sunday evenings.[^cite_note-353]&#x20;

[WBEZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBEZ) produces nationally aired programs such as [PRI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Radio_International)'s *[This American Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_American_Life)* and [NPR](29697232.md)'s *[Wait Wait...Don't Tell Me!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wait_Wait...Don%27t_Tell_Me!)*.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355]&#x20;

## Infrastructure

### Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Chicago)



[DuSable Lake Shore Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Shore_Drive)



[Jane Byrne Interchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jane_Byrne_Interchange)

Chicago is a major transportation hub in the United States and North America. It is an important component in global distribution, as it is the third-largest inter-modal port in the world after [Hong Kong](13404.md) and [Singapore](27318.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMadigan200452-356]&#x20;

The city of Chicago has a higher than average percentage of households without a car. In 2015, 26.5 percent of Chicago households were without a car, and increased slightly to 27.5 percent in 2016. The national average was 8.7 percent in 2016. Chicago averaged 1.12 cars per household in 2016, compared to a national average of 1.8.[^cite_note-357]&#x20;

#### Parking

Due to Chicago's [wheel tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheel_tax),[^cite_note-358] residents of Chicago who own a vehicle are required to purchase a Chicago City Vehicle Sticker.[^cite_note-359] In established Residential Parking Zones, only local residents can purchase Zone-specific parking stickers for themselves and guests.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361]&#x20;

Chicago since 2009 has relinquished rights to its [public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_area) street parking.[^cite_note-362] In 2008, as Chicago struggled to close a growing budget deficit, the city agreed to a 75-year, \$1.16 billion deal to lease its [parking meter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parking_meter) system to an operating company created by [Morgan Stanley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_Stanley), called [Chicago Parking Meters LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Parking_Meters_LLC). Daley said the "agreement is very good news for the taxpayers of Chicago because it will provide more than \$1 billion in net proceeds that can be used during this very difficult economy."[^cite_note-fail-363]&#x20;

The rights of the parking ticket lease end in 2081, and since 2022 have already recouped over \$1.5 billion in revenue for [Chicago Parking Meters LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Parking_Meters_LLC) investors.[^cite_note-364]&#x20;

#### Expressways and tollways

Main article: [Roads and expressways in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_and_expressways_in_Chicago)

Eight mainline and four auxiliary [interstate highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System) ([41](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_41) (mostly in [Wisconsin](33127.md)), [55](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_55_in_Illinois), [57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_57), [65](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_65_in_Indiana) (only in [Indiana](21883857.md)), [80](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_80_in_Illinois) (also in [Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_80_in_Indiana)), [88](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_88_\(west\)), [90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_90_in_Illinois) (also in [Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Toll_Road) and [Wisconsin](33127.md)), [94](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_94_in_Illinois) (also in [Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_94_in_Indiana) and [Wisconsin](33127.md)), [190](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_190_\(Illinois\)), [290](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_290_\(Illinois\)), [294](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_294), and [355](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_355)) run through Chicago and its suburbs. [I-490](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_490_\(Illinois\)) is currently under construction on the west side of O'Hare Airport. Segments that link to the city center are named after influential politicians, with three of them named after former U.S. Presidents (Eisenhower, Kennedy, and Reagan) and one named after two-time Democratic candidate [Adlai Stevenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlai_Stevenson_II).&#x20;

The [Chicago Skyway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Skyway), which carries I-90, is the only toll road within the city limits and is operated by the Skyway Concession Company. The Skyway leads into the [Indiana Toll Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Toll_Road). The [Illinois State Toll Highway Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Tollway) (known simply as the *Illinois Tollway*), operates several toll roads in the suburbs and throughout Northern Illinois, but none enter the City. [I-Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-Pass) serves as the Illinois Tollway's primary electronic toll collection system. I-Pass and [E-Z Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-ZPass) are both accepted on the Skyway and Indiana Toll Road.[^cite_note-365]&#x20;

The [Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_Expressway) and [Dan Ryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Ryan_Expressway) Expressways are the busiest state maintained routes in the entire state of Illinois.[^cite_note-366]&#x20;

#### Transit systems



[CTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Transit_Authority) [Chicago "L"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_%22L%22) rapid transit



[Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra) commuter rail



[CTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Transit_Authority) bus

The [Northern Illinois Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois_Transit_Authority) (NITA) coordinates the operation of three transit service boards that service the City and the suburbs: CTA, Metra, and Pace. All three of NITA's service boards provide a combined 1.3 million rides a day over 7,200 miles of bus and rail routes as of 2026, making it the second-largest [public transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport) system in the United States.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]&#x20;

- The [Chicago Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Transit_Authority) (CTA) handles public transportation in the City of Chicago and a few adjacent suburbs outside of the Chicago city limits. The CTA operates an extensive network of buses and a [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) elevated and subway system known as the [Chicago "L"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_%22L%22) (or just the "L") (short for "elevated"), with lines designated by colors. These rapid transit lines also serve both [Midway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Midway_International_Airport) and [O'Hare](248449.md) Airports. The CTA's rail lines consist of the [Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Line_\(CTA\)), [Blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Line_\(CTA\)), [Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(CTA\)), [Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Line_\(CTA\)), [Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_Line_\(CTA\)), [Purple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purple_Line_\(CTA\)), [Pink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_Line_\(CTA\)), and [Yellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_Line_\(CTA\)) lines. The Red and Blue lines both offer 24‑hour service which makes Chicago one of a handful of cities around the world (and one of three in the United States, the other are New York City and Philadelphia) to offer rail service 24 hours a day, every day of the year, within the city's limits.

- [Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra) (NITA's Commuter Rail Division), one of the busiest commuter rail systems in the United States, operates eleven [commuter rail](6286.md) services out of four rail terminals in downtown Chicago. It connects the City with suburban Cook County and the five [collar counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collar_counties) of [DuPage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuPage_County,_Illinois), [Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kane_County,_Illinois), [Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_County,_Illinois), [McHenry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McHenry_County,_Illinois), and [Will](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_County,_Illinois). One line continues onto [Kenosha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenosha,_Wisconsin), [Wisconsin](33127.md).

  [^cite_note-369]

  &#x20;Starting in 2027, there will be a [new inter-city service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford_Intercity_Passenger_Rail) to [Rockford, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford,_Illinois).

- [Pace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pace_Suburban_Bus) (NITA's Suburban Bus Division) provides [bus](4146.md) service primarily in the suburbs with some routes entering the City. It also provides [paratransit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paratransit), [demand-response](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demand-responsive_transport), and [vanpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanpool) service for the entire NITA region, including the City.

  [^cite_note-370]

[Ventra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventra) serves as the fare payment system for CTA and Pace. Metra tickets are also sold on the Ventra mobile app.[^cite_note-371] A monthly Regional Connect Pass or Regional Day Pass can be purchase alongside a Metra ticket, allowing for unlimited rides for all three agencies.[^cite_note-372]&#x20;

The [Northern Indiana Commuter Transportation District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Shore_Line) (NICTD) operates the South Shore Line, which provides commuter rail service from [Millennium Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Station) to [Northwest Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Indiana). Since March 2026, it consists of two services: the [Lakeshore Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakeshore_Corridor) to [South Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bend_International_Airport_station) and the [Monon Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monon_Corridor) to [Munster/Dyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munster/Dyer_station).[^cite_note-373]&#x20;

#### Inter-city bus

The [Chicago Bus Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bus_Station) serves as the city's primary inter-city bus terminal. It is served by carriers such as [Greyhound Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greyhound_Lines), [Flixbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FlixBus), [Megabus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megabus_\(North_America\)), and [Burlington Trailways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burlington_Trailways). Some inter-city bus services stop at other locations such as O'Hare Airport and Union Station with carriers such as [Amtrak Thruway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Thruway), [Coach USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coach_USA), [Peoria Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_Charter_Coach_Company), and [Van Galder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Galder_Bus_Company). Tornado Bus Company stops in [Little Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lawndale,_Chicago) and provides bus connections to Mexico.[^cite_note-374] In June 2026, the City purchased the Chicago Bus Station for \$19.2 million.[^cite_note-375]&#x20;

#### Passenger rail



[Chicago Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station) is [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak)'s hub for both national long-distance trains and inter-city trains in the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Midwest). It is also served by six [Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra) commuter lines.

[Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) long distance and [inter-city rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-city_rail) services originate from [Chicago Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station).[^cite_note-376] Chicago is one of the largest hubs of passenger rail service in the nation.[^cite_note-377] The services terminate in [Port Huron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Huron,_Michigan), [St. Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Paul,_Minnesota), the San Francisco Area, New York City, New Orleans, [Portland, Oregon](23503.md), [Seattle](11388236.md), [Miami](53846.md), [Milwaukee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee), [Carbondale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbondale,_Illinois), [Quincy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy,_Illinois), Boston, [St. Louis](27687.md), [Kansas City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri), [Grand Rapids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Rapids,_Michigan), [Los Angeles](18110.md), [San Antonio](53848.md), and [Pontiac, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontiac,_Michigan). Future service will terminate at [Moline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moline,_Illinois).&#x20;

Chicago has a total of four active rail terminals: [Chicago Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station), [Ogilvie Transportation Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ogilvie_Transportation_Center), [LaSalle Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LaSalle_Street_Station), and [Millennium Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Station). Amtrak only serves Union Station, while Metra serves all four stations. The South Shore Line services uses Millennium Station as its Chicago terminal.[^cite_note-378]&#x20;

#### Bicycle and scooter sharing systems

In July 2013, the [bicycle-sharing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle-sharing_system) [Divvy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divvy) was launched with 750 bikes and 75 docking stations.[^cite_note-379] It is operated by [Lyft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyft) for the [Chicago Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Department_of_Transportation).[^cite_note-380] As of July 2019, Divvy operated 5800 bicycles at 608 stations, covering almost all of the city, excluding [Pullman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pullman,_Chicago), Rosedale, [Beverly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly,_Chicago), [Belmont Cragin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belmont_Cragin,_Chicago) and [Edison Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edison_Park,_Chicago).[^cite_note-381]&#x20;



[Divvy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divvy) bikes

In May 2019, The City of Chicago announced its Chicago's Electric Shared Scooter Pilot Program, scheduled to run from June 15 to October 15.[^cite_note-382] The program started on June 15 with 10 different scooter companies, including scooter sharing market leaders [Bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird_Global), [Jump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jump_\(transportation_company\)), [Lime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lime_\(transportation_company\)) and [Lyft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyft).[^cite_note-383] Each company was allowed to bring 250 [electric scooters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_motorcycles_and_scooters), although both Bird and Lime claimed that they experienced a higher demand for their scooters.[^cite_note-384] The program ended on October 15, with nearly 800,000 rides taken.[^cite_note-385]&#x20;

#### Freight rail

The Chicago Terminal is the largest and busiest rail hub in North America.[^cite_note-386] All six [Class I railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class_I_railroad) and two “switcher” railroads ([BRC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_Railway_of_Chicago) and [IHB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Harbor_Belt_Railroad)) meet in Chicago,[^cite_note-387] with 27 major intermodal rail yards in the region.[^cite_note-388]. 25% of all [freight trains](70161362.md) and 50% of all [intermodal freight trains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermodal_freight_transport) in the United States passes through Chicago.[^cite_note-389] As of 2002, severe freight train congestion caused trains to take as long to get through the Chicago region as it took to get there from the West Coast of the country (about 2 days).[^cite_note-390] According to U.S. Department of Transportation, the volume of imported and exported goods transported via rail to, from, or through Chicago is forecast to increase nearly 150 percent between 2010 and 2040.[^cite_note-391] The [Chicago Region Environmental and Transportation Efficiency Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Region_Environmental_and_Transportation_Efficiency_Program) (CREATE), comprises about 70 programs, including crossovers, overpasses and underpasses, that intend to significantly improve the speed of freight movements in the Chicago area.[^cite_note-392]&#x20;

#### Airports

Further information: [Transportation in Chicago § Airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Chicago#Airports)



[O'Hare International Airport](248449.md)



[Midway International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midway_International_Airport)

Chicago is primarily served by [O'Hare International Airport](248449.md) (ORD), the world's busiest airport measured by airline operations,[^cite_note-393] on the far Northwest Side, and [Midway International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midway_International_Airport) (MDW) on the Southwest Side. Both airports are owned and operated by the [Chicago Department of Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Department_of_Aviation) (CDA).[^cite_note-auto-394]&#x20;

O'Hare is major hub for [American Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines) and [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines). As of 2025, O'Hare is the world's busiest airport by aircraft movements.[^cite_note-395] As of August 2026, O'Hare has over 1,100 daily domestic flights to nearly 200 destinations in the United States and over 140 daily flights to nearly 70 international destinations.[^cite_note-396] The CTA's [Blue Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Line_\(CTA\)) provides direct rail access to O'Hare from downtown 24 hours a day, seven days a week.&#x20;

Midway is a major operating base for [Southwest Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Airlines). As of July 2026, Midway has over 250 daily domestic flights to over 80 destinations in the United States.[^cite_note-397] There are also international flights to Canada, Mexico, and the Caribbean. The CTA's [Orange Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Line_\(CTA\)) provides direct rail access to Midway from the [Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Loop_\(CTA\)).&#x20;

[Gary/Chicago International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary/Chicago_International_Airport) and [Chicago Rockford International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Rockford_International_Airport), located in [Gary, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary,_Indiana) and [Rockford, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford,_Illinois), respectively, can serve as alternative Chicago area airports. However, they do not offer as many commercial flights as O'Hare and Midway. In recent years the state of Illinois has been leaning towards [building an entirely new airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_Chicago_south_suburban_airport) in the Illinois suburbs of Chicago.[^cite_note-auto-394]&#x20;

The City of Chicago is the world headquarters for [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines), the world's third-largest airline.&#x20;

#### Port authority

Main article: [Port of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Chicago)

The Port of Chicago consists of several major port facilities within the city of Chicago operated by the Illinois International Port District (formerly known as the Chicago Regional Port District). The central element of the Port District, Calumet Harbor, is maintained by the [U.S. Army Corps of Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Army_Corps_of_Engineers).[^cite_note-398]&#x20;

- Iroquois Landing Lakefront Terminal: at the mouth of the Calumet River, it includes 100 acres (0.40 km

  2

  ) of warehouses and facilities on Lake Michigan with over 780,000 square meters (8,400,000 ft

  2

  ) of storage.

- Lake Calumet terminal: located at the union of the [Grand Calumet River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Calumet_River) and [Little Calumet River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Calumet_River) 6 miles (9.7 km) inland from Lake Michigan. Includes three transit sheds totaling over 29,000 square meters (310,000 ft

  2

  ) adjacent to over 900 linear meters (3,000 linear feet) of ship and barge berthing.

- Grain (14 million bushels) and bulk liquid (800,000 barrels) storage facilities along [Lake Calumet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Calumet).

- The Illinois International Port district also operates [Foreign trade zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_trade_zones_of_the_United_States) No. 22, which extends 60 miles (97 km) from Chicago's city limits.

### Utilities

Electricity for most of [northern Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois) is provided by [Commonwealth Edison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Edison), also known as ComEd. Their service territory borders [Iroquois County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iroquois_County,_Illinois) to the south, the [Wisconsin](33127.md) border to the north, the [Iowa](26810748.md) border to the west and the [Indiana](21883857.md) border to the east. In northern Illinois, ComEd (a division of [Exelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exelon)) operates the greatest number of nuclear generating plants in any U.S. state. Because of this, ComEd reports indicate that Chicago receives about 75% of its electricity from nuclear power. Recently, the city began installing wind turbines on government buildings to promote renewable energy.[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401]&#x20;

Natural gas is provided by Peoples Gas, a subsidiary of [Integrys Energy Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrys_Energy_Group), which is headquartered in Chicago.&#x20;

Domestic and industrial waste was once incinerated but it is now [landfilled](211285.md), mainly in the [Calumet area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Calumet). From 1995 to 2008, the city had a [blue bag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_bag) program to divert recyclable refuse from landfills.[^cite_note-402] Because of low participation in the blue bag programs, the city began a pilot program for blue bin recycling like other cities. This proved successful and blue bins were rolled out across the city.[^cite_note-403]&#x20;

### Health systems



Prentice Women's Hospital on the [Northwestern Memorial Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Memorial_Hospital) Downtown Campus

The [Illinois Medical District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Medical_District) is on the Near West Side. It includes [Rush University Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rush_University_Medical_Center), ranked as the second best hospital in the Chicago metropolitan area by *U.S. News & World Report* for 2014–16, the [University of Illinois Medical Center at Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_College_of_Medicine), Jesse Brown VA Hospital, and [John H. Stroger Jr. Hospital of Cook County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_H._Stroger_Jr._Hospital_of_Cook_County), one of the busiest trauma centers in the nation.[^cite_note-404]&#x20;

Two of the country's premier academic medical centers, [Northwestern Memorial Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Memorial_Hospital) and the [University of Chicago Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago_Medical_Center), reside in Chicago. The Chicago campus of Northwestern University includes the [Feinberg School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feinberg_School_of_Medicine); Northwestern Memorial Hospital, which was ranked as the best hospital in the Chicago metropolitan area by *U.S. News & World Report* for 2017–18;[^cite_note-405] the [Shirley Ryan AbilityLab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirley_Ryan_AbilityLab) (formerly named the Rehabilitation Institute of Chicago), which was ranked the best U.S. rehabilitation hospital by *U.S. News & World Report*;[^cite_note-406] the new [Prentice Women's Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Northwestern_University_buildings#Prentice_Women's_Hospital); and Ann & Robert H. Lurie Children's Hospital of Chicago.&#x20;

The [University of Illinois College of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_College_of_Medicine) at UIC is the second-largest medical school in the United States (2,600 students, including those at campuses in Peoria, Rockford and [Urbana–Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_at_Urbana%E2%80%93Champaign)).[^cite_note-407]&#x20;

In addition, the [Chicago Medical School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Medical_School) and Loyola University Chicago's [Stritch School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stritch_School_of_Medicine) are located in the suburbs of [North Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Chicago,_Illinois) and [Maywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maywood,_Illinois), respectively. The [Midwestern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_University) Chicago College of [Osteopathic Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osteopathic_medicine_in_the_United_States) is in [Downers Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downers_Grove,_Illinois).&#x20;

The [American Medical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Medical_Association), [Accreditation Council for Graduate Medical Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accreditation_Council_for_Graduate_Medical_Education), [Accreditation Council for Continuing Medical Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accreditation_Council_for_Continuing_Medical_Education), [American Osteopathic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Osteopathic_Association), [American Dental Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Dental_Association), [Academy of General Dentistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_General_Dentistry), [Academy of Nutrition and Dietetics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Nutrition_and_Dietetics), [American Association of Nurse Anesthetists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Association_of_Nurse_Anesthetists), [American College of Surgeons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_College_of_Surgeons), [American Society for Clinical Pathology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Society_for_Clinical_Pathology), [American College of Healthcare Executives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_College_of_Healthcare_Executives), the [American Hospital Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Hospital_Association), and [Blue Cross and Blue Shield Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Cross_and_Blue_Shield_Association) are all based in Chicago.&#x20;
